This ledger records local implementation evidence. It does not represent owner approval, publication authorization, deployment, or a production-readiness decision.

## Current cycle

- Mandatory cumulative baseline: `v116-cumulative-reality-v1` (balanced, high, and 390×844 recordings preserved); T1, Q2, and Candidate F remain earlier accepted subsystem baselines.
- Selected evaluated candidate: `v116-cumulative-reality-as`, with natural-duration `balanced`, `high`, and `mobile` recordings under one evidence root.
- Accepted cumulative subsystems: everything selected through AR. AS changes only the shared analytic lake-boundary scale by adding three broad near-bank coves and six far-bank cove/spur fields with a bounded `0.72` minimum. Active Valley terrain, live lake water, the continuous submerged basin bed, and the per-column river/lake solve consume the same boundary. Camera, duration, topology counts, ecology, atmosphere, coast, waterfall, world assets, streaming, fallback, and prerecorded-video exclusion remain unchanged.
- Iteration evidence preserved: AS1 moved the near bank but left the far bank and pointed eastern leaf too smooth; AS2 strengthened the near bank without clearing the platter read; AS3 established useful far-bank coves/spurs but retained the pointed eastern terminus. AS adds the bounded eastern recession; no detached shoreline mesh, foam line, shader disguise, camera move, asset, or alternate renderer was introduced.
- Whole-world decision: **not accepted as photoreal or production-ready**. AS raises only the exact unbroken oval/pointed lake-planform P0 from score 1 to 3 at natural 27.5-, 35-, and 41-second timing. Overall water and terrain remain 2, mobile composition remains 2, reference realism remains 1, and no physical-device or production-performance claim is made.
- Identical evidence timing: opening; 5, 15, 18.8, 22.5, 27.5, 35, 41, 45.5, 49.5, and 53.7 seconds; then Ocean/About, Projects/Sky, and Contact.

## Perceptual scorecard

Scores require standardized recorded-frame support. Empty cells are intentionally unscored rather than inferred from asset counts.

| Category | v1.16 baseline | Candidate F | Candidate T1 | Evidence |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Photographic terrain | 1 | 1 | 2 | F/Q2 opening, Valley, Waterfall, and Summit frames replace flat faceting with higher-density geometry and full PBR breakup; broad authored forms remain obvious |
| Vegetation realism | 1 | 2 | 2 | Candidate F’s safe detailed balanced vegetation is retained; T1 extends replacement-based real trees into the lake zone but the aerial delta is not enough for score 3 |
| Canopy density | 1 | 2 | 2 | T1 visibly increases grounded mobile ecology and adds lake-zone real-tree replacements; large exposed slopes and mobile sparsity remain |
| Ecological layering | 1 | 2 | 2 | More existing grounded instances improve mobile continuity, but the procedural ground layer and shoreline habitat remain simplified |
| Water realism | 1 | 1 | 2 | Q2 lake/river material is retained and T1 adds darker multi-scale animated ocean swell with analytic normals and Fresnel sky response; polygonal banks and the coastline edge cap the score |
| Waterfall realism | 1 | 2 | 3 | Q2 replaces the planar veil and separated strips with an irregular curved plunge surface, animated breakup, impact rings, and rising spray visible in desktop/mobile motion; cliff geology remains simplified |
| Atmosphere and sky | 1 | 2 | 3 | Q2 establishes a stronger blue zenith/horizon, less fog wash, and clearer land/sky/ocean separation while retaining one authority; clouds remain procedural |
| Lighting and color | 1 | 2 | 3 | Warmer key plus cooler fill reveals brown/green geology and avoids F’s uniform dark-green response; some ravines remain compressed |
| Scale and depth | 2 | 2 | 3 | PBR terrain, corrected outcrop scale, blue aerial separation, and curved fall geometry improve depth cues across identical cameras |
| Camera composition | 2 | 2 | 2 | Identical 54-second path retained; several views remain too high/wide and mobile still clips some landmarks |
| Motion continuity | 4 | 4 | 4 | Full journey plus Ocean, Sky, and Contact completes in all three playable T1 recordings using the live WebGL renderer |
| Artifact cleanliness | 2 | 2 | 3 | Rejected slab, separated waterfall strips, stacked rocks, broad plane, and corrugated water are absent; polygonal shoreline/world-edge defects remain |
| Desktop composition | 2 | 2 | 3 | Terrain material and atmosphere establish clearer focal hierarchy, though the world is not photographic |
| Mobile composition | 2 | 2 | 2 | Stable full sequence, improved waterfall/sky, and visibly denser grounded ecology; conservative terrain and sparse procedural forms still cap the score |

## T1 cycle stop boundary

This implementation cycle ends at T1 because it adds two defensible, recorded gains without weakening Q2 or overstating the whole world: the live ocean surface now reads as moving water rather than a flat plane, and mobile-visible grounded ecology is denser at zero added asset-transfer cost. The accepted source excludes the failed shelf rings, fake foam, camera evasions, and escarpment disguise. All required balanced/high/mobile recordings and exact-state build/runtime gates exist. Further material progress requires remeshing the source terrain boundary into a genuine coast, authored irregular lake/river banks with localized habitat, denser mobile-safe species forms, and physical-device measurement—not another surface shader or camera workaround.

## V1 cycle stop boundary

V1 is the current accepted cumulative candidate because it converts the largest terminal-scene failure—a missing rectangular section of world—into connected live terrain without changing the camera, using video, fetching a new asset, or regressing earlier standardized views. It is deliberately capped at coastline score 2. Further material progress should replace the procedural shoulder and compact seam with a genuinely remeshed coast, then address polygonal inland banks, mobile-safe terrain density, cloud structure, and physical-device profiling. More shader camouflage around the present seam would not justify a higher score.

## W2 cycle stop boundary

## X4 cycle stop boundary

X4 is the selected local cumulative candidate because it closes one genuine score-1 P0—the compact terrain's dominant quantized surface artifact—to score 3, and it improves the live lake/terrain interface without adding a bank ring, video path, asset transfer, camera evasion, or mobile v1.15 load. ## Public release baseline and Candidate BI validation

- Commit `87a36d848cfe1073b79fafb4c4f22953c8860d20` replaced the public prerecorded homepage path with the live v1.16 renderer while retaining the semantic site, skip path, capability fallbacks, and development-only review route. - BI is the first substantial post-launch cumulative realism batch. It changes five coupled visible categories in one cycle: semantic camera composition, physical environment/light, haze/cloud integration, terrain material response, and canopy material/coverage. It does not add a second renderer, video cover, screenshot plate, detached landscape shell, or fallback asset cost.
- The rail now interpolates against the authored checkpoint parameters rather than equal Catmull-Rom arc length. The public Valley and Lake progress positions therefore hit the authored controls instead of being occluded by the foreground hillside; chapter reporting remains tied to the manifest's semantic progress.
- A generated physical-sky environment now contributes bounded PBR reflections. Direct, hemispheric, ambient, and cool fill light are rebalanced; distance fog and cloud banks separate terrain planes without concealing silhouettes. Terrain albedo is less saturated and orange, normals carry more readable relief, roughness is more physical, and ambient occlusion is less crushed.
- Detailed canopy sources receive deterministic source-key tint variation, lower emissive/environment response, higher foliage roughness, wider crown-only spread, and a modest balanced mid-canopy scale increase. Trunks are not widened with crowns; the compact/mobile source policy is retained.
- The final five-view evidence at `v117-public-reality-a2` records Ridge, Reveal, Lake, Waterfall, and Summit with one live canvas, zero videos, 26 to 29 loaded world resources, and zero page errors. The local public matrix passes 6/6, the shared internal matrix passes 7/7, `pnpm.cmd check` passes ESLint, TypeScript, and optimized Next.js 16.3 build, and the optimized public matrix passes 6/6.

## BL release boundary

BL is selected for release because it simultaneously corrects the public journey authority, meaningful-load start, world continuity, environmental motion, public exploration controls, water residency, and balanced ecological density without weakening any fallback or reintroducing video. It is not a photoreal or finish milestone. The next substantial release must prioritize terrain/geology texture scale, non-repeated ecological source silhouettes, terrain-intersecting weather, and physically stronger lake/ocean/coast optics, followed by real-device profiling.

## Candidate BM validation

- Fresh discovery identified Candidate BL commit `72741257c2f104bf901b416ae9a79cc049934604` as local `HEAD`, GitHub `main`, and Vercel production source. Deployment `dpl_49daMYiHT7j6b1vp55StSBoWMjbJ` was `READY` and promoted at immutable URL `https://projectwebbing-cpdp4kbbj-unigentamos.vercel.app`; Candidate BK deployment `dpl_6VYz6HmPjAXGnh6FB1TEGAhb3rT2` is the verified rollback target. Tracked and staged files were clean before BM; all pre-existing untracked roots remained untouched.
- Visitor-visible hypothesis: replace BL's smooth painted landform/water read with materially finer volcanic source geometry and larger-scale PBR surface variation, then carry the same release through ecological form diversity, permanent watershed/ocean residency, coast optics, atmospheric depth, reachable Summit semantics, and a less obstructive controller.
- BM adds one conforming selective subdivision across 34,118 visible detailed-Alpine crown triangles. The accepted detailed surface grows from 210,304 to 313,292 triangles and deforms 111,557 protected source vertices through real terrace scarps, talus breaks, gullies, the retained asymmetric crowns, headwall, ribs, and drainage. The compact/mobile topology remains its bounded 139,776-triangle path. Outer world edges and the Valley seam remain protected; no shell, skirt, cover card, or detached summit is added.
- Detailed Valley terrain now receives one adaptive lake-bank pass and two waterfall-headwall passes after the retained shared-edge pass. Terrain PBR repeat scale moves from 160/260/300 to 94/116/138, retains the approved Poly Haven maps, and now preserves chromatic source response alongside macro soil/moss/basalt, mineral grain, slope/elevation/moisture, littoral wetness, and crossed fissure fields. Normal response increases without adding a texture or network request.
- Detailed canopy and fern/shrub/rock groundcover gain deterministic per-instance lean, yaw, nonuniform crown width/depth, height, and restrained hue variation. High foliage can cast its alpha-tested canopy shadow; procedural foliage receives a brighter eight-tone habitat range and bounded environmental fill. Existing source and licensing boundaries are unchanged.
- The integrated lake/river/waterfall authority and ocean remain mounted for the complete public rail. This removes the former Summit water unmount and prevents first-render Ocean pop. The ocean shader now discards inland fragments against its analytic coast, separates shoal and deep water, adds moving irregular surf, and extends the physical surface beyond the camera far horizon; inland water adds darker depth grading, submerged-bank variation, and bounded caustic breakup.
- The Summit chapter threshold moves from the unreachable `1.0` frame to `0.94`, so the public rail now enters and reports Summit before its automatic Ocean/Sky holds. The Summit Sky view keeps the mountain silhouette in frame instead of looking almost vertically into an empty dome. Cloud/sky depth and fog balance are strengthened, and the public journey controller contracts from 44 to 38 rem with lower opacity while retaining every labeled control and gesture instruction.
- Two local sub-candidates were rejected before selection. The first permanently resident ocean rendered inland because it lacked a coastline discard; the second experimental anisotropic cirrus field produced large sky streaks. BM retains the residency and deeper sky color but excludes both defects.
- Re-reviewed 2026-09-01 references: NPS Haleakala elevation/ecosystem zones for altitude/moisture distribution; the registered CC0 Na Pali helicopter photograph for eroded ridge silhouette and canopy mass; the Wailua Falls reference for source/lip/plunge relationships; and the Matterhorn/Stellisee reference for mountain-water separation and reflection. These remain view-only research references; no reference media was copied into the product.

Candidate comparisons use BL's production sequence at `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/production-sequence-72741257/sequence.json`.

The public local matrix passes 6/6: supported desktop/mobile keep one canvas and automatic Ridge-to-Reveal movement, while reduced-motion, data-saver, low-power, and no-WebGL policies keep zero canvas/video/world requests. The internal development matrix passes 7/7, including the 313,292-triangle detailed crown invariant, bounded compact Alpine path, v1.15 comparison, reduced motion, fallback, and no-v1.15 mobile policy. ## BM selection boundary

BM is selected because a normal visitor can see simultaneous improvement in crown relief, rock/ground scale, foliage-form variation, complete water residency, ocean horizon/surf, Summit/Sky composition, and control restraint. Its structural work is genuine source-surface topology rather than camouflage, and the complete functional/fallback contract passes locally. The result is still plainly synthetic and does not pass the final realism gate: large terrain faces remain coarse; repeated source families and dark foliage remain obvious; the lake, river, and waterfall lack full reflection/refraction/foam detail; terrain-intersecting cloud and valley mist remain weak; mobile ecology is comparatively sparse; and physical-device profiling is absent. After production verification, the next dominant batch should replace repeated canopy silhouettes and add spatially grounded near/mid/far ecological layers while coupling tree contact, terrain shading, and localized terrain-contact weather across Ridge, Valley, Lake, and Waterfall.

## Candidate BN validation

- Fresh discovery on 2026-09-02 found local `HEAD`, GitHub `main`, and the public alias at final Candidate BM metadata commit `83898e0f3e32c71238da085735cd1285e05d1f79`. That metadata deployment was `dpl_5vTLcjspnRtHFuUxXF8rPrdaKTEb`; the underlying verified BM implementation and bounded rollback target were commit `ddef01340250cd1d4348fbee5cc85a6ddbf4e184` and deployment `dpl_GpShLzpKGg6cRM3g7ZZv8iKfN1QZ`. Tracked and staged files were clean before BN. All pre-existing untracked asset, artifact, renderer-source, and Chrome-tooling roots remained untouched.
- Visitor-visible hypothesis: replace the repeated same-height dark canopy with a deterministic emergent/mature/umbrella/wind-pruned/columnar hierarchy, ground the released understory in the actual ridge-drainage and eastern-valley catchment networks, and carry the same release through foliage/light response, flatter lake optics, visible cloud depth, softened terrain contrast, and a less obstructive opening composition.
- The structural ecology change keeps detailed v1.15 vegetation as an anchor stratum instead of replacing every eligible primary-canopy slot. Balanced desktop deterministically retains 76 percent of Ridge, 74 percent of Valley, and 80 percent of Alpine anchor placements; the released positions remain occupied by the existing ten grounded procedural species families. Production telemetry records 930 released primary-canopy positions at Ridge/Valley and 938 after Lake activation.

- Lake vertical displacement and the authored ripple normal are reduced. ## BN selection boundary and next dominant batch

BN is selected because a normal visitor can see cumulative improvement in six categories and because its core ecology change is a genuine hierarchy tied to accepted terrain/habitat authorities. The release preserves the continuous automatic world, one-canvas/zero-video authority, Ocean/Sky exploration, fallbacks, compact resource boundary, and verified motion. It does not meet the realism gate. The next dominant batch must attack the largest remaining still-image gap: replace legacy card-like primary tree sources with visibly richer near/mid/far crown and trunk silhouettes, break the broad faceted terrain through structure-preserving material/normal scale and grounded rock/soil transition, add basin-reflection and shoreline depth cues, and establish terrain-contact mist/weather across Ridge, Valley, Lake, and Waterfall. Physical-device profiling remains a separate mandatory gate before any performance claim.

## Candidate BO validation

- BO retains BN's five terrain-coupled canopy architectures, 930 to 938 released primary-canopy slots, 349 drainage/catchment-grounded understory placements, flatter lake response, cloud depth, continuous automatic journey, permanently resident watershed, Ocean/Sky controls, capability fallbacks, compact resource boundary, and one-canvas/zero-video renderer authority.
- The dominant normal-view defect was not raw placement count but the dark, flat, repeated detailed foliage source response. BO preserves one trunk per grounded placement while expanding primary foliage in six suitable families into deterministic two- or three-lobe crowns. Production telemetry records 670 volumetric crown placements and 1,928 rendered crown lobes at Ridge/Valley, rising to 715 placements and 2,026 lobes after Lake activation. Compact/mobile remains single-lobe and adds no resource.
- Detailed foliage now preserves substantially more of each source's own albedo instead of multiplying it by a gray-green tint. A restrained green emissive fill, lower environment intensity, high roughness, and lobe-specific deterministic color separation produce the largest visible BO gain: vegetation reads green and layered across Opening, Valley, Lake, Waterfall, Summit, Ocean, and Sky rather than as black or silver cards.
- The retained terrain shader derives a small world-scale fBm gradient and mixes it into the view normal according to surface slope and terrain zone. This creates structure-preserving micro-relief without changing topology or adding a detached shell, card, skirt, cap, or texture request. Broad landform facets and material repetition remain visible and are not disguised by this change.
- Lake water receives only a bounded 0.14 ripple-normal contribution, low-amplitude moving surface variation, stronger sky reflection, and a small glint. The v3 shoreline-opacity/feather experiment created a black luminous rim and was fully reverted; the selected v4 retains BN's stable shoreline authority. Basin shape, hard shore transition, reflection/refraction, and depth cues remain visibly authored.
- Four low-opacity animated contact-weather banks are aligned to the existing ridge-drainage, eastern-valley-catchment, waterfall-plunge, and lake-basin authorities. They are excluded from the conservative/mobile tier. Exact telemetry proves their presence and fixed-camera hashes prove scene motion, but production visual review finds the mist deliberately subtle rather than a major terrain-cloud realism promotion.
- The public landing wordmark contracts from BN's `9.6vw/10.2rem` ceiling to `7.2vw/7.8rem`, exposing materially more of the opening world while leaving the semantic site, navigation, journey controls, automatic start, and swipe/pointer affordances unchanged.
- Three overcorrections were rejected before selection. `candidate-bo-v1` retained the gray canopy response and was too visually modest; `candidate-bo-v2` left detailed foliage silver and dark; `candidate-bo-v3` fixed foliage color but introduced the lake-rim artifact. `candidate-bo-v4` is the selected corrected composite.
- Selected local evidence is `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/candidate-bo-v4/sequence.json`; the exact production-to-production board is `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/candidate-bo-production-comparison.png`. Visual inspection confirms greener, deeper crown mass and the smaller landing obstruction across all seven views. It also exposes the unchanged photographic gap: repeated legacy tree geometry, large faceted terrain, limited ground-transition detail, a hard-edged lake, simplified waterfall/coast optics, and weak terrain-weather contact.
- Fresh `pnpm.cmd check` passes ESLint, TypeScript, and the optimized Next.js 16.3 build. Development internal runtime passes 7/7 at `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/candidate-bo-final-dev/internal-runtime-gates.json`; development public runtime passes 6/6 at `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/production-public-runtime/candidate-bo-final-dev/runtime-gates.json`; optimized public runtime passes 6/6 at `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/production-public-runtime/candidate-bo-optimized/runtime-gates.json`; optimized seven-view and fixed-camera motion evidence pass under `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/candidate-bo-optimized/`.

## BO perceptual delta

Scores are visible-result judgments at ordinary 1440x900 viewing size, not implementation or test scores. Candidate BN is the direct production baseline; BO is the selected cumulative release.

| Category | Candidate BN | Candidate BO | Decision boundary |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Detailed foliage color/material legibility | 1 | 3 | Source-albedo recovery and calibrated green indirect fill remove the dominant black/silver card response across every exact view. |
| Primary crown volume and silhouette breakup | 1 | 2 | Deterministic two- and three-lobe crowns add near/mid-field mass and height variation, but they still reuse the same legacy source geometries. |
| Opening-world exposure | 3 | 4 | The smaller wordmark reveals substantially more terrain and ecology without removing identity, navigation, or controls. |
| Terrain micro-surface response | 1 | 2 | Zone- and slope-aware world-scale normals add fine shading variation; broad faceted landform geometry and repeated color fields remain dominant. |
| Lake optical variation | 2 | 2 | Ripple normal, surface variation, reflection, and glint are safer and more alive, but the retained hard shoreline and shallow optical model prevent promotion. |
| Terrain-contact weather | 1 | 1 | Four grounded banks exist and move, but they remain too subtle in the selected production views for a visible-score promotion. |
| Overall ecology | 3 | 3 | BO improves color and crown relief materially, but source repetition, limited age/species silhouettes, sparse compact ecology, and weak ground contact remain. |
| Motion and semantic continuity | 4 | 4 | Ridge and Ocean pixels change with the camera rail and scroll held; all seven semantic views remain reachable in one live canvas with zero videos. |
| Reference realism | 1 | 1 | Photographic references remain decisively ahead in terrain scale, source diversity, canopy density, shoreline/reflection integration, terrain-cloud contact, and dynamic range. |

## BO selection boundary and next dominant batch

BO is selected because it delivers simultaneous production-visible improvement in foliage legibility, structural crown depth, opening composition, terrain micro-response, water motion/reflection, and grounded weather infrastructure while retaining every public journey and fallback contract. It is not a finish claim. The next dominant release cannot be another tint, particle, or single-tree correction: it requires a licensed, provenance-recorded near/mid/far vegetation source rebuild with materially different trunks, branching, crown silhouettes, age classes, and ground-contact shadows; a structurally authored terrain/shoreline transition with rock, soil, scree, erosion, and shallow-water depth cues; and a stronger terrain-intersecting weather/reflection pass tested on real devices. Those asset/source changes require a separate bounded provenance and optimization cycle rather than silently absorbing the pre-existing untracked asset roots. Until that work exists, reference realism remains `1/5` and the site remains a live, functional, cumulative public prototype rather than a photoreal finished world.

- Visitor-visible hypothesis: attack BO's broad painted Valley faces and hard lake bank together by adding protected flank-scale volcanic relief, stronger climate/material breakup, a non-black shallow-water edge, more legible lake motion, and materially stronger terrain-contact weather in one cumulative batch.
- The active detailed Ridge retains five hierarchical drainage trees but adds two second-order rills per tree, stronger unequal buttresses, and bounded terrace breaks. Runtime telemetry records 98,082 adjusted Ridge vertices, 10.393873 metres maximum incision, and 4.741882 metres maximum uplift while preserving the Ridge-to-Valley and outer world boundaries.
- The active Valley source gains two unequal flank-scale interfluve systems, secondary shoulders, narrow runoff incisions, crossing rills, and weathered terraces. It modifies 219,888 existing refined vertices, reaches 12.962127 metres maximum incision and 6.269461 metres maximum uplift, and explicitly protects the lake, river, waterfall, Ridge boundary, and Alpine boundary. No detached shell, skirt, terrain card, shoreline collar, or cover geometry is added.
- The selected Valley remains 744,535 triangles / 373,066 vertices. An early adaptive-subdivision experiment inflated the same mesh to 2,715,849 triangles because its broad pass fed the later waterfall refinements; it produced no proportional visible gain and was rejected. The public sequence and internal runtime verifier now enforce a 1.5-million-triangle ceiling for this authority.
- Terrain PBR response reduces inherited source-texture authority, adds weathered mantle/depositional breakup, elevation-warped windward/mesic moisture mosaics, and retains slope-, fissure-, basalt-, litter-, moss-, and littoral-coupled response. The intent is to reduce the large single-color painted sheet without hiding the true source geometry.
- The lake keeps the shared analytic surface/bank authority but replaces the near-black visible basin bed with muted wet basalt, raises edge optical density, strengthens irregular moving surface variation, and restores bounded wind/glint response. The selected fixed views remove the prominent black shoreline rim while preserving the connected inlet, river, waterfall, plunge pool, outflow, coast, and ocean chain.
- Terrain-contact weather expands from four to six animated banks. Ridge drainage, Valley catchment, waterfall plunge, and lake basin gain modestly stronger contact; upper-Valley saddle and coastal-shoulder authorities add depth where BO remained empty. Conservative/mobile still excludes the banks, and no fog wall is used to conceal geometry.
- Two foliage experiments were rejected. The first darker palette plus balanced alpha-shadow path crushed canopies into black silhouettes; a lighter intermediate palette remained visibly too dark. Candidate BP restores BO's accepted foliage response exactly rather than claiming an ecology improvement it did not earn.
- Re-reviewed references remain view-only: the NPS Haleakala ecosystem description for windward moisture and elevation bands, the USGS Hawaiian-volcano evolution overview for porous basalt and deep erosional valleys, and the USGS Kauai landscape account for narrow ridges, deep canyons, and weathered volcanic structure. No reference media or new asset enters the product.
- Selected local seven-view evidence is `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/candidate-bp-selected/sequence.json`; the topology optimization comparison is `output/playwright/madagin-world-progress/candidate-bp-selected/comparison-topology-optimization.png`. Every view records one canvas, zero videos, `scrollY=0`, 28 to 29 world resources, zero page errors, six terrain-contact authorities, and exact watershed telemetry. Fixed-camera Ridge and Ocean pairs differ after 1.6 seconds with journey progress and scroll held.
- `pnpm.cmd check` passes ESLint, TypeScript, and the optimized Next.js 16.3 build. Development internal runtime passes 8/8, including the new bounded watershed-relief case; development public runtime passes 6/6; final optimized `next start` public runtime passes 6/6 and its fixed-camera Ridge/Ocean motion proof passes.
- Source SHA-256 before metadata release: renderer `E603495AB5E8E84937EF4113FFC83463BFE770C152735A3955DE9E1F0F07CD75`; public sequence verifier `865721294FCC639B78783E35EAD0D02A7D87B4663F56BFA322E694E54441ED46`; internal runtime verifier `1ADD88C26D8D6D9A5A5D0289853FDC480E3F5DC25F570EC250A0D93A96AA57F8`.

## BP perceptual delta before production release

Scores are ordinary 1440x900 visible-result judgments, not implementation or test scores. Candidate BO is the direct public baseline.

| Category | Candidate BO | Candidate BP | Decision boundary |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Regional Valley landform hierarchy | 1 | 2 | Protected interfluves, shoulders, runoff incisions, cross-rills, and terraces break part of the single broad plane; the inherited macro-scale silhouette is still visibly authored. |
| Ridge second-order erosion | 2 | 3 | Each of the five retained drainage trees gains bounded second-order rills, stronger unequal buttresses, and terrace breaks on the active source surface. |
| Terrain material/climate breakup | 2 | 3 | Weathering, deposition, reduced source authority, and windward/mesic mosaics add materially broader surface variation without a new texture or shell. |
| Lake shoreline/depth presentation | 1 | 2 | The black basin rim is removed and the edge has denser muted shallow-water response; boundary scale and reflection/refraction remain simplified. |
| Lake environmental motion | 2 | 3 | Stronger non-repeating surface variation, wind response, and glint remain live while the physical boundary ring stays fixed. |
| Terrain-contact atmosphere | 1 | 1 | Six grounded banks replace four and occupy two missing corridors, but fixed views still do not justify a photographic terrain-cloud score. |
| Overall ecology | 3 | 3 | BO's accepted foliage/crown system is preserved after two darker BP experiments failed; repeated legacy tree families and weak ground contact remain dominant. |
| Continuity / fallbacks | 4 | 4 | One autoplaying canvas, zero videos, all seven views, four zero-world-request fallbacks, and compact no-v1.15 policy remain intact. |
| Reference realism | 1 | 1 | The world remains plainly synthetic beside reference photography, particularly in source terrain resolution, tree repetition, water reflection/refraction, coast scale, and dynamic range. |

## BP selection boundary

BP is selected locally because it combines genuine protected source-surface landform change, broader physical terrain response, a repaired lake edge, stronger live lake optics, and expanded grounded weather while rejecting both a black-canopy regression and a 2.72-million-triangle overbuild. It is not a photoreal or finish milestone. The next dominant batch must replace repeated near/mid/far tree sources and ground-contact lighting, materially rebuild the inherited large-scale ridge/Valley silhouette and lake/coast optical model, strengthen visible terrain-intersecting cloud without concealment, and collect real-device performance evidence.

## BP released boundary and next dominant batch

BP is released because it delivers one coherent production-visible batch across Ridge structure, regional Valley morphology, material/climate breakup, lake-edge correction, live water response, and terrain-contact weather without regressing the continuous public journey, one-canvas/zero-video authority, accessibility fallbacks, compact resource boundary, or bounded topology. This is not the convergence endpoint. The next accepted release must produce a larger normal-view gain by replacing the repeated near/mid/far vegetation sources with licensed provenance-recorded trunk, branch, crown, age, and shadow diversity; rebuilding the most visible inherited mountain and shoreline macroforms on protected shared surfaces; adding materially more credible shallow-water depth, reflection/refraction, surf, and coast scale; strengthening terrain-intersecting cloud and atmospheric depth without hiding geometry; and collecting physical-device smoothness and thermal evidence. Micro-calibration remains subordinate to those visible structural gains.

- Bounded rollback remains Candidate BQ deployment `dpl_G9hb7RfkzohAMSY9Pot5PzkK8BdP` at `https://projectwebbing-kxoud4vk8-unigentamos.vercel.app`, source commit `86d65a97a5d1e172a975ff81cc305edd96d1cf15`; it was re-inspected as `READY` immediately before the release.

## BR released boundary and next dominant batch

BR is released because it fulfills BQ's stated source-quality boundary with a provenance-recorded, directly rendered four-form tree source and a visible correction to the dominant black canopy while simultaneously advancing retained terrain, lake, and atmospheric response. The next substantial candidate should add genuinely different licensed species/age/branching families through bounded near/mid/far assets, raise the visible terrain and shoreline source resolution without detached cover geometry, replace the authored lake/coast/waterfall optics with stronger physical reflection/refraction/plunge/surf response, make cloud contact read at terrain scale without concealment, and collect actual phone/laptop smoothness and thermal evidence.

## BS perceptual delta before production release

Scores are ordinary 1440x900 visible-result judgments, not implementation or test scores. Candidate BR is the direct production baseline.

| Category | Candidate BR | Candidate BS | Decision boundary |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Source-quality geology | 0 | 2 | Thirty-four grounded anchors now use seven verified scanned forms; the batch adds real local shape and material diversity but does not rebuild the inherited macro-landforms. |
| Geological grounding | 1 | 2 | The selected small, brighter, deeply seated forms avoid BS-01's conspicuous perched-prop regression; broad slopes still lack natural exposed strata, scree fields, and erosion-scale rock continuity. |
| Terrain material scale | 3 | 3 | Lower UV-face authority and moderated normals reduce broad painted facets, but the low-resolution source faces remain dominant in Valley and Alpine views. |
| Lake false-pattern suppression | 2 | 3 | Much lower cross-wave/reflection amplitude removes the strongest diagonal/radial streak field while preserving transparency; reflection, refraction, depth, banks, and surface scale remain authored and simplified. |
| Terrain-contact atmosphere and lighting | 2 | 2 | Brighter physical fill and slightly stronger six-bank contact improve readability, but clouds still do not merge with landforms at photographic scale. |
| Source-quality vegetation | 2 | 2 | BR's 40 four-form Pachira anchors remain intact; BS adds no new plant species, age class, or crown architecture. |
| Motion and semantic continuity | 4 | 4 | Fixed-camera Ridge/Ocean pixels change, all seven views remain available at zero scroll, and every fallback preserves its zero-world-request boundary. |
| Reference realism | 1 | 1 | The release reduces two obvious synthetic signals but remains plainly synthetic beside photographic terrain, vegetation, water, coast, and weather references. |

## BS released boundary and next dominant batch

BS is released because it adds a second verified source-quality runtime path, corrects a visible lake-pattern defect, and advances terrain material scale plus atmospheric readability without regressing BR vegetation, the connected watershed, source-surface ownership, automatic journey, one-canvas/zero-video authority, compact policy, or fallbacks. It is not a finish milestone. The next substantial candidate should prioritize a provenance-recorded second vegetation family with genuinely different trunks, branching, crowns, and age classes; materially higher-resolution shared mountain/shore/coast source surfaces rather than detached cover geometry; more physical reflection/refraction, depth, plunge, and surf response; terrain-intersecting cloud at visible scale without concealment; and actual phone/laptop smoothness and thermal evidence.

## Candidate BT validation

- Fresh discovery on 2026-09-03 found local `HEAD`, GitHub `main`, and the current public alias at Candidate BS metadata commit `54633ce007ffdcb8ef35eb1a30f1f167f5a504e1`. Vercel deployment `dpl_HJ4HPXQdq32LF8QYB31oE5su69tW`, immutable URL `https://projectwebbing-o820ah6f4-unigentamos.vercel.app`, and that exact source commit were independently `READY` and alias-attached before implementation; this is BT's bounded rollback identity. The tracked tree was clean and the large pre-existing untracked asset/review corpus remained user-owned and untouched except for the four explicitly selected derivatives and their provenance record.
- Visitor-visible hypothesis: make one structural release across the most obvious scene-wide failures by replacing face-oriented terrain-map dominance with world-projected detail, cutting the inherited oversized oval lake into a smaller shared volcanic tarn, eroding and relaxing the synthetic summit crown, adding a genuinely different coastal-tree silhouette family, and deepening humid sky/water separation without moving the public camera or hiding geometry.
- The terrain shader now applies the retained ground texture after the standard map stage through triplanar world projection. The old decoded normal atlas is removed because its coarse UV islands reproduced face-sized shading discontinuities; bounded world-space micro-normal response remains. AO and source-albedo authority are reduced while damp basalt, moss, sediment, and material-scale response remain slope/moisture aware.
- Detailed Alpine keeps the existing 313,292-triangle protected source surface but replaces the dominant artificial uplift with four descending shoulders around two negative amphitheaters, a broad headwall, and drainage. A five-iteration typed-array heightfield relaxation adjusts 277,700 samples with a hard 5.5-metre per-step ceiling after incision, while the Valley boundary and world bounds remain protected. The selected result is less conical and less jagged than the rejected structural intermediates, but the inherited faceted silhouette is still visibly synthetic.
- One shared lake authority changes from approximately `152.816 x 118.075` metres to `132.4 x 94.6` metres. Terrain carve, basin bed, water surface, banks, inlet, and shoreline selectors all consume that same smaller footprint; the shader deepens absorption, lowers decorative surface mixing, and strengthens broken reflection. The complete river, waterfall, plunge, outlet, coast, and ocean chain remains protected.
- BT promotes a second licensed vegetation family from the already reviewed Poly Haven Island Tree 03 scan. The raw approximately 79 MB / 5-million-triangle source is not shipped; four reproducible 512px orthographic derivatives total 1,121,316 bytes and mount as two crossed planes at 26 Ridge and 34 Valley grounded balanced/high-desktop placements. Compact/mobile and Alpine remain excluded. The exact source, texture, and derivative SHA-256 hashes, authors, retrieval date, CC0 license, and Blender reproduction script are recorded in `world-source/assets/polyhaven/island_tree_03/PROVENANCE.md`.
- The sky/fog palette gains stronger humid depth and more visible moving billows while retaining one physical light environment and no fog concealment layer. The public seven-view comparison shows a clearly smaller/better seated lake and a changed summit/sky hierarchy, but the new tree family is a secondary silhouette gain rather than a dominant photographic transformation.

All seven views record one canvas, zero videos, `scrollY=0`, zero page errors, Candidate BT identity, 60 Island Tree placements, the `132.4 x 94.6` shared tarn authority, and every retained Pachira/geology/watershed invariant.

## BT perceptual delta before production release

Scores are visible-result judgments at ordinary 1440x900 size, not implementation, topology, asset-count, or test scores. Candidate BS is the direct production baseline.

| Category | Candidate BS | Candidate BT | Decision boundary |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Shared lake/basin scale | 1 | 2 | The visibly oversized oval becomes a materially smaller shared tarn and exposes more coherent Valley floor; shore geometry and reflection/refraction remain authored. |
| Alpine macroform | 1 | 2 | Negative amphitheaters and bounded relaxation reduce the isolated pyramid/jagged uplift, but coarse inherited faces and an artificial crown remain obvious. |
| Terrain material scale | 3 | 3 | World projection removes the strongest face-oriented atlas behavior and reduces painted facets across views; the source landform and broad material families still read as procedural. |
| Source-quality vegetation diversity | 2 | 2 | A distinct wind-shaped scan derivative is verified at 60 placements, but its normal-view visual contribution is modest and crossed planes are not a near-camera photographic solution. |
| Water optics and continuity | 2 | 2 | Deeper broken reflection and one smaller shared footprint improve integration without breaking topology; the waterfall strip, lake optics, and ocean/surf remain plainly authored. |
| Atmosphere and light | 2 | 2 | Deeper sky/fog separation and more visible cloud structure improve layering, but landform-scale cloud contact remains weak. |
| Motion and semantic continuity | 4 | 4 | Fixed-camera pixels change in Ridge and Ocean; the complete journey and all truthful fallbacks retain their accepted behavior. |
| Reference realism | 1 | 1 | The cumulative delta is real but small beside photographic references; broad faceting, repeated ecology, simplified water, waterfall, coast, and weather remain dominant. |

## BT local selection boundary

BT is selected locally as an intermediate cumulative release because the production-identical comparison shows a consequential lake-scale correction plus multi-view summit, terrain-surface, sky, and bounded ecology changes, while every functional and fallback gate remains intact. It is not photoreal, finished, owner-approved, or physical-device validated. The next substantial batch must attack the remaining dominant visual failures rather than tune BT's labels: rebuild the waterfall/lake/river optical and plunge transition, add real shoreline/rock/sediment structure on shared surfaces, materially improve coastal/ocean contact and surf scale, replace more repeated near/mid vegetation silhouettes without increasing compact cost, and strengthen terrain-intersecting weather. Physical phone/laptop smoothness and thermal evidence remains an explicit final-gate requirement.

## BT released boundary and next dominant batch

BT is released because it makes one production-visible structural correction to the oversized inherited lake while simultaneously advancing summit source geometry, terrain material scale, licensed vegetation diversity, water response, and atmosphere without regressing the continuous journey or truthful fallbacks. It is not the convergence endpoint. The next accepted release should rebuild the visibly weakest water/terrain relationship: waterfall lip, curtain shading and breakup, plunge contact, river/lake sediment transition, shore material structure, and coast/surf contact, together with a further bounded silhouette-diversity and terrain-intersecting-weather pass. The final gate also remains blocked on real phone/laptop thermal and smoothness evidence.
